From this low origin has sprung the whole secret 1
lodge system. WitnesB the four old masonic lodges in
four little Inns in London viz: " The Gooee and Grid- '
iron, " Crown and Anc/tor,'" "Rummer and Grapes;"
and "Api»lL'tree Tavern" in 1717. These four formed
tiho first Grand Lodge. Satan, "the god of this world,"
kindles the fires of false religion al the bottom of the
human grate. Ninety five years after these low Inn
Clubs formed the first devil worshiping Grand Lodge in
London , the same sort of drinklng-ulubs formed the first
Odd-fellows Lodge in Manchester, England. The name
they chose, Odd-fellows, shows their vulgar and rowdy
origin. This was in 1612; only seven years later, (1619)
nios. Wildey, inoculated the United States, with this
peligious epidemic, in a Baltimore tavern and it spread
like small pox. Wildey's substitute for Christianity
was "to improve and elevate the character of man,"
( Grosh't Man. p. SI, by making able bodied white men,
above 21 years of age " Odd-Jellows;" stealing from the
Bible, but omitting Christ; while believingin a "Supreme i
Being. "-( Wltite's Dig. p. J,S.)

first: — UISTOKY OP THIS DBGBSE.

A few years since, aa Odd-fellowsbip was getting
familiar and stale, observing that the war had made the
false "Grand Army," and Knights Templar popular;
some Odd-fellows invented atlniformed Odd-fellowship
which they called The Patriarchal Circle, and initiated
some who were not Odd-fellows. The thing took and
Circle set up for indojiendiinGe of the Sovereign


A PROFITABLB SWINDLa

Oraod Lodge TLis Circle charged $30 for its cliartoi
and tlO for four copies of its ritual and was doing a

thriving business, which, of course, cut into the revenues
of the Odd-feilow8, and war at once arose between the
Sovereign Grand Lodge and the "Supreme Temple,"

Some Encampment Oddfellows, in the "Patriarchal"
degree of which system the candidate represfnts iHaau
and his attersptcd Bacrifice by Abraham on Mount
Moriah, met in Wisconsin to contrive a ITnifor
the Knights Templar. Learning that some Rhode IbI;
and New Hampshire Patriarchs had already Invented
one, they sent for and adopted it; this was in 1
Two Grand Sirea of the Odd-fellows forbid, by procli
tion, wearing that uniform. Grand Sire Stuart '
to Providence to abolish the uniform. The G
Patriarch and Scribe of Bhode Island, met the G
^ire oi ihe Lodge of the World at a tavern, doubtless
took something to drink; and gut him into an opeoj
carriage and the Patriaroha, with the brave unifoi
which he had forbidden them to wear, marohed before'
him through the City and back to hiH Hotel. "Well"
boys said Grand Sire Stuart, "il is Bnc, but yon must
not do it." Seeing the cap, plume and gauntlets were
to be the rage, and the Circle would beat the Grand
Lodge, Odd-fellowa, who were also in the Patriarchal
Circle, attempted to merge the Circle in this new degree
which the Odd-fellows were getting up, called "Th^
Patriarchs Militant," to swallow up the "Supi
Temple" of the Circle in the Sovereign Grand Lodge o\
the Odd-fe!low8. This attempt to merge the Circle in
the lodge was made at a special session of the Supreme
Temple in Chicago, November 1885. It was defeated
by ^es, 25; Noea, 37. — So the Sovereign Grand Lodge
and Supreme Temple are still (1S80) running head aud^

Mormons, Knights Templar, Grand Army of the
Republic, Armed Secret Socialists and Dynamiters, The
Patriarchal Circle; and now this Patriarchs degree, rest^
ing on half a million tif Odd-fellows, are turning these
United States into a nation of Secret Guerrilla bands,
armed and drilling nightly, sworn to obey orders and
waiting for something to do! And while this writing
proceeds, news comes of a new Secret Order of "Minute
Men," started by a preacher, Edmonston of Sedalia, Mo.,
professedly to swear the friends of "law and order" in
the United States, to aid in suppressing "Strikes and
Boy:otta"by armed secrecy, oaths, signs and grips, and, in
a-few weeks this new secret order reports seventeen Uiou-
aand members. As tho daily papers inform us that large
nambers of Knights of Labor, Freemasons and Odd-
fellows have joined ihia new order and as it is given out
that the order proposes to investigate the causes of strikes
and boycotts, and oppose them if they are unwarranted.

there is no reasonable donbt but the real purpose
organize an armed force to eustain Strikes and Boycotts,'
while professing their object to be to regulate them.
Another secret order against Strikes is jast started
Chicago, called " ConservatovB of Order,'^ to concentrate.
and aid in suppressing laborers' strikes wherever the]
may occur. Of course they must be armed.

Now these secret "law and order" bands, the Sovereigal
Grandljodge of Odd-fellows included, exist in violation
of the laws, which forbid bands of men to uniform, drill
and parade, unless they belong to our open, organized
Militia, and when their work is completed, "law and
order" are at an end. Is not this Military outburst the
flaming sword which spanned the heavens of the Hebrew
Commonwealth before it fell? We have only to
suppose some miscount in a Presidontal Election; some
Mormon outbreak and secret league with a politiual
party suoh as Joe Smith attempted, and partly accom-
plished with Stephen A. Douglas, or some spark from
the laborer's anvil, to ignite these magazines of political
and military dynamite, and we shall have a war of
internal factions, such as made the blood run ankle deep
from the principal gate of Jerusalem; and such as kept
the French guilotine wet, and the rivers of France red in
the reiga of terror under Robespierre, Couthon and St.
Just.

Not counting Christians outside; if American chareh
members, or one half of them, can be enlightened and
brought to "vote as they pray," for God, the Sabbath and
the Bible; impenitent and ungodly men cannot and will-
not rule this Country: — Oaths will again become sacred
and jurors honest. Our Presidcntal Campaigns will be-
come, like the campaign of John Knox in Scotland^
which overturned Popery witiUovA e^wiAding a drop

And if history repeats itself, if rejecting Christ from
their religion sunk the Hebrew Cum mon- wealth, this
sudden burst of night drilling lodges, Mormons, Grand
Army, Knights Templar, Patriarchal Circle, and this
degree of Patriarchs Militant; with a reserve corps of
Secret Dynamiters behind, swearing the same obedience
to they know not whom: — these are not only facts hut
precureora. They are the cohorts which Josephua tella
ua were aeen in the olouds above Judea and the flaroiug
Bword which hung over Jerusalem before Titua burned
their temple; and that wonderful nation went down by
"rapine, murder, famine and pestilence within; and fire,
BWOrd and all the terrors of war without," (Jos. p. 557.)
When "the people fed on one another, and even ladies
broiled their sucking infants and ate them."

Juat fifty years ago when the white haired Dr. Lyman
Beeohor taught us that the terrible predictions of Christ
and bis Apostles would be fulfilled on American soil;
his words seemed to ua harmless vaticination. But a
brief quarter of a century passed and Julia Ward Howe's
"Battle Hymn of the Republic" showed theae prophetic
pictores turned to real life and set them to music whose
echoes still live:

Mine eyes have seen the glorj ot the coming o( the Lord,

Be 1b lieadiag ont hia vintage where tbe grapes ol wrath are Itored.

Yet how few of the million who have sung those
words saw in them the literal fulfilment of the prophecy
of John, (Rev. 14, SO.) When the Angel oast "the vine
of the earth into the great wine press of the wrath ot
God, and blood came out of the wine press."

There is but one preventive of a repetition of these
horrors; that is the repentance, fasting and prayer which
saved Nineveh. This will save ua. The Hebrews never
turned from their idol vanitiea, to the altars where Chript
was worshipped astheirMcssiah, but their enemies made
peace with them; their Common-wealth was restored and
the hills and valleys of Judea rang with the joyoas
anthems of that "happy people whose God is the Lord."

This Military Degree shall be open to Patruirabi of
vhe Royal Purple Degree everywhere, the conditloo r»-
quiBite for receiving or retaining membership being good
Btanding in a. regular Encampment of the Order, without
reference to juriB diction. Thufi an applicant for enroll-
ment on the roster of a Canton shall furnish only a
certificate of membership and good standing in his En-
campment, and make application in the form prescribed
by law, and it shall be received and considered, regard-
less of the jurisdiction from which he hails; but it shall
require a three-fourths (f ) ball vote of the members pr efr
«iit at the time the ballot is taken to uleot ^"


Instructions.


The word to he given at the outer door is the Counte
sign, selected annually by the grand Sire and promulgi
ted by the Lieutenant General.

When the Commandant of a Canton is absent, tl
Lieutenant will assume command and take theCaptainl
tent; the Ensign will lake the Lieutenant's station; an*
the acting Commandant will appoint a temporary Ofiioei
of the Guard, etc. ,

V?hen the Commandant of a Grand Canton is absent,
the next Field Officer in rank, or the senior Captain

§ resent, will assume command, and take the Cotnmai
ant's tent, etc.

Should all the commissioned officers be absent, i
Benior retired officer present will take the Commandant^
tent, etc.

In. floor work by a Grand Canton, the officer next id
rank to the Commandant will take the Lieutenant's tentg
and the next in rank the Ensign's station; and all be a
dressed by their proper military titles.

The uniform to be worn in this degree must confort.
to the regulations adopted by the Sovereign Grand Lodra
of the I. O. O. F., and the floor movements must bi
executed after the manner of ritualistic requirements, i

When the Canton is open and at work, it will bf
necessary for each Chevaber present to be clothed in thj
stipulated uniform, or as may be modified by prbviaionj
of law.

In petitioning a Canton for membership, it is neoei
sary to annex to the application for membership, a certlfl
cate from the Encampment of which the applicant is a
member, setting forth the fact that be is a Patriarch a
the Royal Purple Degree in good standing.

The elective officers of a Canton are a Captain,
Lieutenant, and an Ensign, cunimisBioneJ; and a Clet


and an Accountant, non-commissioned. They hold
office for one year, and until their Bucceasors shall be
mastered and installed.

The appointed officers are a Standard-Bearer, a Guard,
a Sentinel, and a Picket, who rank in the Canton in the
order named. They are appointed by and hold their
positions at the will of the Commandant.

The quorum for a Canton is nine (8) Chevaliers pres-
ent.

Balloting on applications for membership shall be by
ball ballot.

The manner of voting (except upon an application for
membership) shall be Yea or No.

There should be a circular tent at the stations of Cap-
tain and Lieutenant.

(For other and detailed law, see Organization, regula-
tions, etc.)

(In the arrangement of this work, founded upon the
narrative contained in the fourteenth chapter of Genesis
of the Old Testament, the Ritualistic charges, lectures,
obligations, etc., were prepared by John H. Albin, Grand
Rep. from New Hampshire; the Military features, in-
cluding Plates showing floor manfeuvrea, etc., Forms for
opening and closing a Canton, for the installation of
officers, for mustering a Canton into service, for the
burial of a Chevalier, etc., were prepared by Jno. C.
Underwood, Grand Rep. from Kentucky.)

KoTK — In all the military manceuvree that I have pre-
pared, I have adopted the movement by "three8"inatead
of by "fours," as the number of files to be. made the
ordinary unit for breaking into column. Not that I pre-
fer such file unit, where numbers are unlimited, but with
a small command a much greater display can thereby be
made. Thus; 24 file will break from line into column
of 8 Bets of "threes," or 6 sets of "fours." Now the
casual observer will never notice the alight difference iu
the width of the column, but will very likely count the
nnmber of "threes" as they pass; and, consequently, the
display is practically one-lourth greater.


In order to make the greatest possible display with
tlic fewest men, I have dispensed with the "file closers,"
and place the Lieutenaat and Ensign on the right andi
Ipftofthe line, and use them, if necessary, as guidf-
AIbo, that the Canton may always appear to advantaj
in public, I have designated 24 file and 3 officers, 27
all, as tile minimuia number to be allowed to torn out
and parade as a Canton on display occasions.

In the arrangement of special military movements for
display purposes in a hall, some lioesse should be allowed
in the formation of bodies, position of oflicerB, and in
distances. Where double ranks are used in the follow-
ing ritual mantBuvres, the distances have been extended
for floor effect (see Diagrams 4, 5, 6 and 8;) however,
the !<ingle rank movements (Diagram 7) are easier and
equally handsome.

In the Tactics, approved and promulgated, the
Chevaliers fai,i, in with swords at a "Carry;" but in
the floor manoeuvres, I desire the effect of the display
derived from the jfesA of steel, obtained by drawing the
swords simultaneously. Hence the command to Draw^ —
Swords, and return— Swoeds, ^ven to Canton in line.

To execute the movements hereinafter described, the
Canton should be counted off by sixes. For formations
of Canton (except in special movements and positions
of Officers and Chevaliers, hereinafter explained by text
or diagrams,) and plate representations of Officers, eto.,
see Diagram 1.

ORDER OF BUSINESS.


1. Call ro8t«r of ofBcers.

2. ReaA minutes of ihe last Cantonment; which must
be declared approved unless objections are made, in
which ease, errors (if any) must be corrected, and then
the minutes must be approved and signed.

UonB for membership.

Lieutenant — Alas, what a strange contrast to
usually quiet life of a Patriarch, this state of thinj
presents. In "peace or war" he is equally
earnest in the discharge of his duties. In timus of
peace, his attention is taken up with his flocks and herdi
ho 18 then thoroughly engrossed in rural pursuitf
doing good among his fellow-men. His sheep know
voice and seek his presence. The needy and distresi
reverence him. He mingles with them in their jo;
sympathizes with them io their sorrows, and never alloi
them to go empty-handed £iom b\a lent,. But


rtnnATioir. 305

of war all is changed. The Patriarch then becomes
tlioi'ougbly armed and equipped; be submits to and aids
in enforcing the most rigid military discipline. He
never resorts to arma for piirposes of mere personal
aggrandizement. His conflicts are always waged for tbe
redresa of injustice, or for the establishment of some
high moral principle; and at the termination of the con-
test, he quietly returas to his flocks and herds, and re-
sumes tbe life of the noble Patriarch. His crowning
motto is "Universal Justice."

INITIATION. 319

SECOND OBLIGATION PATKIARCHS MILITANT.

, kneeling within this Crown, formed of

living witnesses, do solemnly promise, declare and avow,
that I will never, in any manner wbatever, communicate
or eanse to be communicated, any of the signs, secrete,
mysterieB, rites, written or nnwritten work of the
Patriarchs Militant, to any person or persons whomsoever,
imless he or they be legally entitled to receive the same
from me; that I will promptly and cheerfully obey the
oommandB of my euporior officers in this or any other
Canton to which I may be attached; and will at all
times render to them that respect which their official
position entitles them to receive at my hands; that I will
constantly use my best endeavors to promote harmony,
peace, and concord within the ranks, always avoiding
discord and strife. For the faithful performance of all
these obligations, I pledge my sacred honoras a Patriarch
of the Royal Purple Degree, as well as whatever military
rank or preferment I may hereafter receive through the
medium of this degree.

Commandant — Sir, you have now attained the highest
and most exalted rank in Odd Fellowship. Step by step
you have been deservedly advanced to this high and ,
honorable distinction, and I am gratified that you hs^^


been found worthy lo be enrolled as one of our number,
The true soldier, engaged in a just warfare, presaea for-
ward and strikes valiantly against the stronghold of the
adversary, never yielding until that adversary capitulates.
He is noteasily turned aside; he feara no danger,he heeds
no voice excepting that of hia commander; and when
the conflict is ended, he is equally ready to grant quar-
ter and regard the rights of those who can no longer
protect themselves.

So you, as a soldier in our cause, must always earnest-
ly contend for those reforma best calculated to promote
the moral welfare and advance thecondition of mankind.
Upon all questions affecting the good and well-beijig of
the community in which you live, you must have an
opinion of your own, which you should fearlessly ex-
preaa whenever occasion may require; yet at all times
bearing in mind that others have an equal right -to en-
tertain honest opinions of their own.

On taking the obligation, you were required to kneel,
in token of obedience to those in authority, and of prop-
er humility; you were required to place your right haad
ovpr your heart, lo indicate the aiocerity of your purpose
to faithfully adhere to your sacred pledge of honor; and
your left band was made to grasp the naked blade of
the sword, in order to teach you that justice will sooner
or later overtake you, should you disregard your obliga-
tions.

Tour inability to satisfactorily prove yourself a Pa-
triarch woald have prevented your admission to our
ranks, and might have resulted in disgraceful conse-
quences to yourself, had it not been for the timely inte^
ference of a friend who interceded for you.

Learn from this, that you should condemn no one
hastily; strive rather, by Itiudly words, to- correct and


I


DEQBEB OP PATRIARCHS HTLITAltf.
counteract the inflnence of errors. Do all in your pow-
er to restore those who are worthy to the confidence and
respect of their associatee, never forgetting that "tJni-
veraalJustice" is the first principle of soldierly manhood,
to be carried into effect, first, by means of peace, but
right of every cause should be enforced, even to

waging of war.

Such is the lesson that we inculcate at this time, ai

trust that its influence eball not be without good eSet

upon your future life, {Addressing Ensign.) Sir, foi

Captain — Chevaliers, we are here to render the last
rite the living may minister to the dead. Man is born
to die. The coffin, the grave, the sepulcher, apeak in
silent language of human mortality. Youth, manbood,
age, weakness, vigor, strength ; poverty, affluence, riches;
humility, pride, arrogance; influence, power, greatness;
are all leveled by the immutable hand of death.

The handiwork of the Creator constantly displays tho
unerring certainty of the end and decay of all things.
The beautiful and exquisite plant creation, from the
gloriea of the Tropics, to the ever agreeable verdure of
the North, alike, fade, wither, and die. So it is with
the human family; the living of to-day become the dead
of to-morrow; and wc, mortals, ean but bow in recogni-
tion of the Divine law, whieh Inevitably brings man to


mother earth. Be who now apeaki to yon, tOMj, *r« he
moves from this sepulcher, be called to enter eternity;
for all in time mnat occupy the silent tomb. The strong
and mighty may, in a moment, be swept into tbe mi-
fathomable void between the living and the dead.

As we behold the remains of our departed companion,
let us, as "Patriarchs Militant," inwardly vow to throw
the cloak of charity over the errors of the deceased
Chevalier, and bury them from recollectioo under an
arch of steel, as we bury the body in this grave; and let
ns monument the memory of his noble worth by herald-
ing the good, great, and knightly deeds only of his
honorable life.

Chaplain — Onr Father, who art the great creator of
all things, who in Thy wise providence does dispense
Universal Justice, we beseech Thee to hear the voice of
Thy creatures here assembled in bumhle supplication.
O God, tbe Almighty Ruler of the univerae, we implore
Thee, in our entire helplessness, to bless and comfort
those whom ithas pleased Thee to add to the number of
tbe disconsolate; bear them up under their great afflic-
tion, and sustain them against despondency.

O Heavenly Father, imbue ua with the conviction of
the uncertainty of life, and of the certainty of death; and
we pray Thee to bless the brethren here prepared to
commit the remains of a companion to their last resting-
iplace on earth. Constantly remind tbem of their obliga-
tions and duties as men and brethren through the various
walks of life; and grant Thy Holy Spirit to those whom
Thou hath spared, and confirm their faith in Thee
forever. Great Commander of Heaven and Earth, pre-
serve the principles and purposes of our beloved Order,
snd defend it in the right; guard it against doing or
flountenancing evil, and lead its members along the
paths of virtue and righteousness. Amen.
